## Context

- T0 writes feedback; T1, T2 and both AOT kinds read it as a FactSnapshot. Baseline-AOT ICs write the same schema (H33). PGO profiles (`.ep9prof`) and TS hints (HINTED) use it too, so the three fact sources are interchangeable inputs (H30).
- Depends on: H2 (slot kinds are ABI bytes), H8 (ShapeIds are run-local; IC keys hold weak shape pointers), H12 (no address identity), H13 (stable indices), H17 (shapes), H18 (elements kinds).
- Depended on by: H29 (profiling), H30 (fact lattice), H33 (AOT feedback rule), H35 (unit header `fact_schema`), H37 (HINTED entries).
- Goals: G2-G4 (one-compare IC guards), G11 (dual-mode contracts from M0).

## Decision

Copied from the plan row: FeedbackVector after 8 calls. v1 slot kinds. CacheIR-like guard/action lists; mono → ≤ 4-way poly → megamorphic stub cache. IC state only in RW data; no code patching in JIT or AOT; optional embedded stub corpus. PGO profiles, hints and Warp-style snapshots share the schema; baseline-AOT ICs write it.

The week-4 decisions below fill the parts the plan defers (kind **fill**; decisions-log §11).

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| K1 | **Slot kinds v0** = the 21 existing `FeedbackSlotKind` bytes 0-20 and names. No ABI bump. `RuntimeId` stays a placeholder (19 ids then; 48 ids, 0-47, since ABI 5: H02-Q5) | Each slot operand maps to one kind. The ABI fingerprint hashes names and bytes, not docs; a rename re-blesses 88 goldens and the corpus digest for no gain |
| K2 | **Runtime record**: one 16-byte entry `[key \| value]` per slot. The key is weak, the value strong. A new entry is `[undefined \| int32 0]` | One entry format for T0, JIT and AOT RW cells (OM-5, H8) |
| K3 | **IC states** (OM-6): key undefined = uninitialized; a shape cell = monomorphic; the hole with a POLY_IC cell (≤ 4 cases) = polymorphic; the hole with an int32 = megamorphic, sticky | V8's polymorphism limit is 4 |
| K4 | **Handlers v0** (OM-7): int32 handlers for own data load and store; IC_HANDLER cells for prototype load, missing property and add-property; array and string length by kind; accessor and dictionary receivers record the state and take the slow path | Covers richards and deltablue (M0 exit 4) |
| K5 | **Invalidation v0** (OM-8): one per-isolate `proto_epoch`, bumped on any structural change of a prototype; prototype-chain handlers store and compare it. M1 replaces it with validity cells. **Built 2026-10-26 (M4-P1 VALIDITY; plan v9 V9-D7, IC_LAYOUT 2): validity cells replace the epoch.** One VALIDITY_CELL (kind 79, 48 B: +8 int32 state (1 valid, 0 invalid), +16 its users list (a weak array of the TRANSITIONS kind), +24 int32 users in the list, +32 int32 depth, +40 int32 the epoch when made) per prototype, in its PROTO_INFO +32 (`crates/ep9_vm/src/validity.rs`); the null prototype's cell (depth 0) never becomes invalid. A cell is made at a recording miss (`validity::cell_for`), only under a valid cell of the next prototype, whose users list it joins; a structural change of a prototype (`shape::note_change`, OM-8) clears its cell and every user cell, recursively, and stops at a cell that is already invalid (it has no valid user). A `PROTO_LOAD`, `MISSING` or 0x24 `ADD` handler keeps the cell of the receiver shape's prototype at +16 (strong; a cell refers to no object, so K7's aim holds), and its hit loads the cell and compares one word. A change of a prototype off the receiver's chain no longer makes a case miss, and nothing counts, so nothing saturates (K18). `GLOBAL` (keyed on two shapes, no chain) and a 0x25 `ADD` keep no cell. A chain deeper than 64 prototypes gets no cell, and its cases are not recorded. The case computation stays no GC point (K14): it reads the cell it sees; if that cell is not valid at `ic::record`, the record makes the current one and walks the chain again, and records the case only if the answer holds. `proto_epoch` still counts (the `Cx` field is frozen until M5-P0, plan v9 V9-A25) and feeds only a debug cross-check that walks the chain at a hit (plan v9 R1). [Revised 2026-10-26, M4-P1 review R-K5-1: the cross-check walks the chain only when some prototype changed since the cell was made (the epoch is saturated or differs from the one the cell keeps, `ic.rs` `cross_check`), so it checks how a change spreads over the users lists, not a missing `note_change` call: a structural change that never calls `note_change` moves neither the epoch nor a cell, and the cross-check skips that hit.] | Correct, about 3 instructions on a hit. M4-P1: one epoch for the isolate made every prototype case miss after any prototype change and saturated (K18); a cell per prototype misses only for the changed chain |
| K6 | **Vector** (OM-12, OM-13): FEEDBACK cell (kind 68) per function, owned by a per-unit strong root range; made on the 8th call or the first JumpLoop budget poll; `--feedback-threshold` 0 = eager, u32::MAX = never; fp-3 keeps new.target | V8 allocates after 8 calls; eager mode lets test262 run the ICs |
| K7 | **GC** (OM-14, plan rule 8): in IC and feedback data every heap reference is weak, except the value half of a feedback entry and an IC_HANDLER's own cell | IC data must not keep shapes or prototypes alive |
| K8 | **FactSnapshot v0** = `ep9_facts`, `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` 1 (below) | Position-independent facts for `.ep9prof` and AOT (H29, H30, H33) |

Per kind (byte, opcodes, runtime record, fact record). "IC" is the K3 entry; "bits" is an int32 bitset in the value word. **Note 2026-10-26 (M4-P0, IC_LAYOUT 2):** the "Runtime record" column is the IC_LAYOUT 1 record. From IC_LAYOUT 2 on, the runtime record of each slot kind is the slot table in the `crates/ep9_vm/src/profile.rs` module docs; the 2026-10-26 rewrite (version) row of the Change log lists the kinds whose record changes (0 at `Construct*` sites, 2, 4, 6, 10, 14, 16, 17, 18, 20).

| Byte | Kind | Opcodes | Runtime record | Fact record |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | Call | 0x5e-0x67, 0x69-0x6b, 0x8f call | callee (weak) or hole / int32 count. **M4-P1 (H16-Q2):** at `Call*` sites the key is the native word of the callee's FunctionInfo (FUNCTION +24; not a cell, so no barrier) or the hole, and the count holds only the sampled calls (the first 64 budget units after each poll, `CALL_WINDOW`); `Construct*` sites keep the constructor (weak) and a `CONSTRUCT_SITE` handler or an int32 count (`construct_ic.rs`) | CallFacts: count, IC state, ≤ 4 FunctionRef, result TypeSet |
| 1 | LoadNamed | 0x21, 0x22, 0x8f load | IC | PropertyFacts: state, ≤ 4 (ShapeDesc, Access), value TypeSet |
| 2, 4, 6 | LoadKeyed, StoreKeyed, DefineKeyedOwn | 0x23; 0x26; 0x27, 0x29 | IC | KeyedFacts: PropertyFacts + KeySet |
| 3, 5 | StoreNamed, DefineNamedOwn | 0x24; 0x25 | IC | PropertyFacts |
| 7, 8, 9 | LoadGlobal, LoadGlobalInsideTypeof, StoreGlobal | 0x10; 0x11; 0x12 | holder shape (weak) / handler | GlobalFacts: BindingSet, value TypeSet |
| 10 | HasKeyed | 0x53 | IC | KeyedFacts (OwnField, ProtoField or Missing) |
| 11 | BinaryOp | 0x2a-0x45, 0x59, 0x5a | - / BinaryHint bits | BinaryHint |
| 12 | CompareOp | 0x4b-0x50 | - / CompareHint bits | CompareHint |
| 13 | TypeOf | 0x48 | - / TypeSet bits | TypeSet |
| 14 | InstanceOf | 0x52 | constructor (weak) / int32 state | TargetFacts: state, ≤ 4 FunctionRef |
| 15 | Literal | 0x6c, 0x6d, 0x6e, 0x70, 0x73 | last shape (weak) / int32 count | LiteralFacts: count, ShapeDesc, ElementsKind |
| 16 | CloneObject | 0x72 | source shape (weak) / handler | CloneFacts: state, ≤ 4 (source, result) ShapeDesc |
| 17 | StoreInArrayLiteral | 0x28 | IC | KeyedFacts (Element cases) |
| 18 | ForIn | 0x8c, 0x8d | - / ForInHint bits | ForInHint |
| 19 | JumpLoop | 0x7d | - / int32 back-edge count. **M4-P1 (H16-Q2):** sampled: only a back edge that polls (it used up the interrupt budget) counts | LoopFacts |
| 20 | CreateClosure | 0x74 | the one closure (weak) / int32 0, 1, 2 | ClosureFacts: none, one, many |

T0 v0 writes only the IC entries of 0x21, 0x22, 0x24, 0x25 and the global sites 0x10-0x12 (P3-IC). **M4-P1:** T0 also writes BinaryOp, CompareOp, TypeOf, the keyed kinds, StoreInArrayLiteral, Call at `Call*` sites, JumpLoop, ForIn and CreateClosure (`profile.rs`, with the H16-Q2 cost rules), and Literal, CloneObject and the Call slot of `Construct*` sites (`literal_site.rs`, `construct_ic.rs`, OBJ-ALLOC); InstanceOf stays empty until IC2 (M4-P3). 0x8f `GetIterator` keeps its load slot empty (I34).

**IC v0 as built (P3-IC, week 4).** The layouts and the handler table are in the module docs of `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs`.

| # | As built | Why |
|---|---|---|
| K9 | **Hit test**: `(key as u32) == word0.shape_id` of the receiver (the cage is aligned, so a shape's low 32 bits are its ShapeId), then the handler. An int32 handler has its kind in bits 0-3 and a byte offset in bits 4-31: `FIELD_IN_OBJECT`, `FIELD_OUT_OF_LINE`, `ARRAY_LENGTH`, `STRING_LENGTH`, `SLOW`, `MEGA` | One compare and one load for an own field; no shape load (I44) |
| K10 | **Cells**: POLY_IC (69, 72 B) = 4 cases (key weak, handler strong). IC_HANDLER (70, 40 B) = +8 kind, +16 epoch, +24 holder or target shape (weak), +32 location (an int32 field handler). Handler kinds: `PROTO_LOAD`, `MISSING`, `ADD`, `GLOBAL`. **M4-P1 (2026-10-26, K5):** +16 is the validity cell (`PROTO_LOAD`, `MISSING`, 0x24 `ADD`; traced strongly), undefined (0x25 `ADD`) or int32 0 (`GLOBAL`) | K7: only handlers are strong (and, from M4-P1, the validity cells they keep, which refer to no object) |
| K11 | **Strings** have ShapeId 0: their case key is `KEY_STRING` (int32 0), and `STRING_LENGTH` checks the kind byte (symbols have ShapeId 0 too). Only `length` is cached on strings. **M4-P3 (IC2, 2026-10-27):** a string's method load (`s.charCodeAt`) is cached too, as a `PROTO_LOAD` or `MISSING` case keyed on `KEY_STRING` whose chain starts at %String.prototype% (with that chain's validity cell); its hit checks the kind byte, so a symbol misses (`crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs:480-483`). Such a case never enters the stub cache (H16-Q3). No IC_LAYOUT bump: the pairing of `KEY_STRING` with these handler kinds uses the IC_HANDLER layout that IC_LAYOUT 2 declared, and no persisted reader has it (orchestrator decision P3-ORCH-8). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P3 integration fixer: "Only `length` is cached on strings" held until IC2 3d7632b] | No shape for strings in v0 |
| K12 | **`SLOW` cases** keep their place and take the generic path with no epoch: dictionary shapes (shared, so they cannot tell own keys), accessors, read-only stores, and the exotic keys of `exotic.rs` (a String wrapper's `length`; an array's `length` as a store target; a function's `prototype`, except a load of one already stored). Array-index keys are never cached | A site that sees such objects does not churn; mega stays for >4 shapes |
| K13 | **Add cases** are checked after the store ran: the new shape must be the receiver shape's child with the key as the next {w, e, c} data field. Prototype-mode (unique) shapes get no add case; a FUNCTION shape without a stored `prototype` gets `SLOW`. An out-of-line add hits only if the SLOTS cell has room (growth is slow). 0x24 compares the epoch; 0x25 does not (CreateDataProperty ignores the chain). A non-extensible object has a dictionary shape (OM-10), so an add case never matches it | One shape compare guards the whole transition |
| K14 | **Miss path**: the case is computed before the generic operation (no GC point, no JS) and recorded only if the operation succeeded (a throw records nothing) | A failed or reentrant operation cannot leave a wrong case |
| K15 | **Global ICs** (stretch, built): a lexical binding is a field case keyed on the lexical record's shape (the TDZ hole misses); an own data property of the global object is a `GLOBAL` cell keyed on the global object's shape whose +24 is the lexical record's shape (weak), so a new `let` that shadows it misses. A found global costs one table scan on the miss path (W4-P1-GLOBAL-ONE-SCAN) | Without it, each global load scans the lexical record and the global object |
| K16 | **Shared slots**: `ic::init_vector` scans the function's bytecode when the vector is made; an IC slot that sites with different names share starts megamorphic. BytecodeGen never shares one; hand-built units (tests) do | One name's handler must never answer another name |
| K17 | **0x22** is keyed on the lookup start (the home object's prototype) and passes its `receiver` register as the receiver (W4-P3-SUPER-SPLIT) | §13.3.7.3 |
| K18 | **The epoch saturates** at `u32::MAX` (W4-P3FIX-EPOCH-SAT). There a miss records `SLOW` for a prototype load, a missing property or a 0x24 add, so every older handler misses and only the generic path runs from then on. The real fix is K5: validity cells in M1. **M4-P1 (2026-10-26):** no case compares the epoch since K5's validity cells; the epoch still saturates, but a miss under it records the real case, so a saturated epoch changes no IC (`tests::ic::a_saturated_epoch_changes_no_ic_result`, `tests::validity::two_to_the_32_changes_saturate_nothing`) | A wrap brought back an old handler's epoch and its stale answer after 2^32 prototype changes |

**FactSnapshot v0** (full text: `crates/ep9_facts/tests/golden/schema.txt`):

```text
header 64 B: magic "\x89EP9FS\r\n" | schema_version | bytecode_abi | unit_hash[32] | source | 0 0 0
             | shape_count | record_count | total_size              (u32 little endian)
shapes:  distinct ShapeDesc {ProtoRef, ObjectKind, flags, capacity u16, [(KeyRef, attrs)]}, ascending
records: (function u32, slot u32, kind u8, payload(kind)), ascending; key = (unit_hash, function, slot)
ProtoRef  = null | intrinsic index (H13) | prototype of FunctionRef | unknown
FunctionRef = js (unit hash, function id >= 1) | builtin (BuiltinId u16)
KeyRef    = string (Latin-1 or UTF-16) | well-known symbol | other symbol
```

- No type can hold a ShapeId, an address, a cell kind id or a closure identity. The VM exporter (`ep9_vm::abi::export_facts`, H16-Q5, M0 month 3) turns a live shape into a ShapeDesc.
- Bitsets (TypeSet, BinaryHint, CompareHint, ForInHint, KeySet, BindingSet) only grow and merge by OR. H30 sets precedence between sources.

## Rules

| Rule | Check | From |
|---|---|---|
| Slot kinds are ABI bytes 0-20; a change bumps `ABI_VERSION` and `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` | `ep9_bytecode/tests/abi.rs`; `ep9_facts/tests/schema.rs` | M0 wk4 |
| A new slot kind does not compile until it has a payload | exhaustive matches: `Payload::of`, `SlotFacts::empty`, the decoder | M0 wk4 |
| Any format change bumps `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` | `schema-history.txt`: SHA-256 of `schema.txt` + the sample bytes; refuses a changed fingerprint even under `EP9_BLESS` | M0 wk4 |
| `ep9_facts` depends on `ep9_bytecode` only; no unsafe | dep-guards check 7; `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` | M0 wk4 |
| One encoding per fact set; the same facts in any build order give the same bytes | `tests/encoding.rs` (two build orders, corrupt input, 3,000 random round trips) | M0 wk4 |
| A record whose kind is not the unit's `feedback_slots[i]` is dropped; another ABI or unit hash drops the snapshot | `tests/validate.rs` | M0 wk4 |
| IC and feedback data hold shapes weakly | `ep9_vm` tests: a dead shape clears its entry, a dead holder misses (`tests/feedback.rs`, `tests/ic.rs`) | M0 wk4 |
| Prototype-chain cases compare `proto_epoch`; a value store to a prototype does not bump it, so a prototype load reads the holder at every hit. **M4-P1 (K5):** they keep the validity cell of the receiver shape's prototype instead; a structural change of a prototype on that chain clears it, a value store or a change off the chain does not | `tests/ic.rs`: a replaced method, a read-only `x` on Object.prototype, a missing property that appears; from M4-P1 `tests/validity.rs`: a change below a cached holder, changes off the chain, realms, the GC of prototypes and of users, no saturation, the depth limit, a random walk for the invariant and soundness | M0 wk4; cells M4-P1 |
| IC hit paths do not allocate or call; T0 fast handlers inline them | `cargo xtask disasm-check` (tail handlers) | M0 wk4 |
| ICs never change test262 results | T262 and T262-EAGER give the same sets | M0 wk4 |
| No code patching: IC state lives in RW data | review of JIT and AOT IC code | M5 / A3 |

## Consequences

- **Gains.** One IC entry and one fact schema for T0, JIT, AOT and profiles. No ABI bump in week 4: the 88 BytecodeGen goldens and `abi-history.txt` stay.
- **Costs.** A ShapeDesc is larger than a ShapeId, and it is a prediction: two live shapes can share one description (for example with an `unknown` prototype), so a consumer always guards. Per-function feedback merges closures (H16-Q1).
- **Follow-ups.** `.ep9prof` (H16-Q5b, A3); the H13 index lists that `intrinsic` and `builtin` refs name (H16-Q4); H30 merge rules.

## Validation

- `cargo test -p ep9_facts`: round trip, golden bytes, history, wrong-kind drop, two build orders. Negative controls on 2026-10-21: a 22nd kind fails to compile in 3 matches; a renamed kind and an encoder change fail the history test under `EP9_BLESS=1`.
- SP-Facts in Spike C (M1): one kernel compiled from a PROFILED FactSnapshot gives identical output at ≥ 90% of live-feedback speed.

## Open questions

| ID | Question | Needed by | Proposed default |
|---|---|---|---|
| H16-Q1 | Per-closure FeedbackCell (V8) or per-function feedback | M1 | Per-function; revisit if realms or contexts make sites polymorphic. **Filled 2026-10-27 (M4-P2 ADR-A0; plan v9 V9-D11): per function, as proposed and as built.** One FEEDBACK cell per FunctionInfo, reached from `FunctionInfo.feedback` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/function_info.rs:74-77`), shared by every closure of the function and by every realm (a FunctionInfo is per isolate, H22). Facts, `.ep9prof` and unit IC cells key a function by (unit hash, function id) (K8; FunctionRef), so one vector per function matches them; a call key never leaves the process ([13 §4.3](../plan/13-execution-modes.md#43-rules) F-R8). The cost (Consequences: closures merge) stays watched by the third "Reopens if" row. No code, test or golden changes |
| H16-Q2 | When T0 records BinaryOp, CompareOp, Call and the other non-property kinds | M1 | M1, with 09 §2.2 "T0 records every H16 slot kind" **Filled 2026-10-26 (M4-P1 PROFILE): from IC_LAYOUT 2, with three cost rules (V9-D6).** `crates/ep9_vm/src/profile.rs` writes the value half (an int32) of BinaryOp, CompareOp, TypeOf, the keyed kinds (2, 4, 6, 10, 17), Call at `Call*` sites, JumpLoop, ForIn and CreateClosure; its module docs hold the table (bits, key half, when). A slot is written only when a bit or state is new. (1) **Implicit int32:** an int32 pair with an int32 result (BinaryOp), an int32 operand (unary), an int32 pair (CompareOp) and a present int32 index of an Array (`GetKeyedProperty`) write nothing; the reader adds those bits to every slot of its kind in a function with a vector. An int32 `+`/`-` that overflows stays implicit; `*`, `/`, `>>>`, `%`, `**` record `INT32_OVERFLOW`. A unary op records no overflow. (2) **Sampled (plan R2 "cold path only"; a V9-D6 waiver):** Call records only in the first 64 budget units after each poll (`CALL_WINDOW`), JumpLoop only at a back edge that polls; counts saturate at `i32::MAX`. The Call key is the native word of the callee's FunctionInfo (FUNCTION cell +24; no barrier, never dies, two closures are one target), or the hole for a second target or a non-FUNCTION callee: not the function cell of the Decision table. (3) **Keyed kinds record on their cold path only**; the key half is left to KEYED (M4-P3). ForIn keeps the receiver shape weakly (with the barrier) and `ForInHint::ANY`. CreateClosure counts to 2 (many). Cost (mac a64, instructions, median of 5, `EP9_HASH_SEED=1`, vs d26c819): t0-loop +0.26%, t0-branchy +0.09%, t0-call +0.05%, t0-prop +0.94%, ci-v0 +0.34%; over 1% (waiver): richards +6.66%, deltablue +1.23%, crypto +7.46%, earley-boyer +2.16%, splay +2.04%, navier-stokes +17.76% (number BinaryOp and CompareOp records, and cold keyed records: keyed is cold until KEYED's fast path). x86-64 is not measured: there 22 recording handlers (`Add`, `Sub`, the compares, `TypeOf`, ...) save one or two registers on entry (`disasm-check -v`); `JumpLoop` and `AddSmi` do not. [Revised 2026-10-26, M4-P1 review R-H16-P1-1 and R-H16-P1-2: against the same tree with every hook empty, the 22 x86-64 handlers save 1 to 4 registers at entry, so the int32 cases pay it too: 4 in `Div`, `DivSmi`, `TestEqualStrict` and the four relational compares; 3 in `ForInNext`; 2 in `Add`, `Sub`, `Mul`, `MulSmi`, `Negate`, `ToNumber`, `ToNumeric`, `TestEqual`, `ForInPrepare`, `CallWithSpread`; 1 in `Inc`, `Dec`, `BitwiseNot`, `TypeOf` (aarch64: no frame change); the x86-64 cost is the orchestrator's decision P1-ORCH-3. The cost list above is against d26c819, where raytrace did not run; against the same tree (33b0eac) with every hook empty, raytrace is +1.38% (mac a64, the same counter), and the waiver is split (P1-ORCH-2b: the keyed part W-PROFILE-1a ends at the KEYED merge; the BinaryOp NUMBER and CompareOp part W-PROFILE-1b is accepted, with a month-5 row).] [Revised 2026-10-26, M4-P1 PROFILE-X64 (P1-ORCH-3): the fast handlers no longer hold record code. A record leaves the handler as a `Flow` exit (`Record`, `RecordPair`, `JumpRecordCall`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/mod.rs`): in the sibling expansion a tail call of an out-of-line function (`record_next`, `record_pair_next`, `record_call_next`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/dsl.rs`) that takes one constant word (the bits, and the slot operand's place) and finds the next pc itself; a Call records after the frame push. TypeOf, ForInNext, CallWithSpread, BitwiseNot and the non-number unary and compare cases record on their cold path; the binary and relational fast bodies are the hooks-off bodies, with the bits chosen before the operation. The facts recorded are the same (`tests::profile`, `tests::feedback`). `disasm-check -v` (5 targets, 284 handlers, 282 tail): the 22 handlers save no register at entry on x86-64 (Linux and Windows), and every target's list of handlers with a frame is the hooks-off list; cold handlers save more (the records moved there). Int32 path, instructions (shortest path from entry to the dispatch jump; hooks off / before / after): x86-64 Linux `Add` 21/26/21, `TestLessThan` 22/31/21, `Inc` 11/13/12; aarch64 `Add` 19/17/17, `TestLessThan` 23/22/19, `Inc` 12/12/10. Cost (mac a64, the same counter, against 16582a4 with every hook empty): t0-loop -2.80%, t0-branchy -3.55%, t0-call -0.03%, t0-prop -0.23%, ci-v0 -0.59%, richards +7.78%, crypto +4.91%, navier-stokes +17.96% (16582a4: +7.07%, +7.46%, +17.62%). x86-64 is still not measured at run time.] [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P1 records review R-RF-1 (the P1-ORCH-3 result): x86-64 cachegrind (sandbox, perf-ci-v0, instructions) at fc77622 against d26c819 / against the hooks-off build of 4c2d101: t0-loop +0.28 / +0.27%, t0-branchy -1.87 / -1.74%, t0-call +0.93 / +0.93%, t0-prop +0.40 / +0.80%, ci-v0 -0.07 / +0.76%, richards +9.29 / +9.44%, deltablue -0.93 / +1.31%. The gate set is within +1.0% on x86-64; richards is the CompareOp record cost of W-PROFILE-1b.] Tests: `tests::profile` (16) in `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/profile.rs`. [2026-10-29, M5-P1 integration fixer, from PROFILE5: (1) since MODES-D16 (M5-P1) an int32 `+`, `-`, `Inc` or `Dec` whose result is outside int32 records `INT32_OVERFLOW` on its overflow arm, so "An int32 `+`/`-` that overflows stays implicit" and "A unary op records no overflow" above describe M4 only (Fact meanings item 2). (2) Since the M5-P1 window jitter a poll resets the budget to `BUDGET` plus 0..127 units, so the Call window is the 64 units after that jitter, not the first 64 after the poll (Fact meanings, sampled counts).] |
| H16-Q3 | Megamorphic stub cache layout | M1 | A per-isolate (ShapeId, key) table flushed at every GC (H8) **Filled 2026-10-27 (M4-P3 IC2; plan v9 §5.3, OI-09): as proposed, with the operation in the key.** `crates/ep9_vm/src/stub_cache.rs`: one table per isolate (`Runtime::stub_cache`), 1024 entries of (key = operation << 32 \| receiver ShapeId, name = the atom's bits, handler), direct-mapped by a hash of the three, in Rust memory. The operation is load (0x21, 0x22), store (0x24) or define (0x25): a field a load reads is not always one a store may write or a define keeps, and a 0x24 add checks the chain while a 0x25 add does not. The handlers are the K9-K13 handlers (smi field and `ARRAY_LENGTH`, `PROTO_LOAD` and `MISSING` with their validity cell, `ADD`), so a hit runs the same handler code as a case of the site's own. Probe: on the miss path (`ic::mega_load`, `ic::mega_store`, called by `slow::get_named`, `get_super`, `set_named` before the generic op), not in the hit path, where it would cost every monomorphic hit two more loads (+1.4% instructions on t0-prop). `SLOW` cases and strings (ShapeId 0) are never stored. Fill: the miss path of a megamorphic entry computes the case for an object receiver (`ic::skip_miss`, `ic::MEGA_FILL`), and `ic::record` puts it in the table, after its last allocation. Weak: the GC does not trace the table; a weak source of the heap empties it in every collection, before the sweep (one flag test when nothing was put in since the last GC), so a ShapeId or atom address that a dead cell frees never matches an old entry. Not exported: compiled code reaches a megamorphic site through `Generic` (CacheIR op lists). Probe (`ic-stats`, mac a64, EP9_HASH_SEED=1): raytrace megamorphic load misses that reach the generic path 1,598,234 -> 368 (all at one site, the anonymous constructor's `this.initialize` of `Class.create`; stub cache hits 1,597,866); instructions -2.2% (raytrace). Tests: `tests::stub_cache` (9). The InstanceOf slot (14) is filled by `crates/ep9_vm/src/instance_ic.rs` in the same phase (IC_LAYOUT 2 INSTANCEOF handler; `tests::instance_ic`, 9). **Note 2026-10-27 (M4-P4 integration fixer A; MODES-CHECK-w15 MC-w15-7a):** the size (1024), the hash and the probe place (the miss path; compiled code through `Generic`) are tuning values in the doc 13 C-25 form, which freeze at the A4 gate, not with this row at M1 exit; the operation set (load, store, define) is append-only; the flush rule is "at least at every major GC" (the table is emptied at every collection today, minor ones included, which meets it). **Note 2026-10-27 (M4-P4 integration fixer C; Codex D2-1):** replaces the flush rule just above. The table is emptied at every collection, minor ones included. A table kept across a minor GC needs first a minor-GC weak filter of every cell an entry names: `ic::record` can make a young IC_HANDLER that only the table names, a minor GC would free it while its receiver shape and name still match, and `ic::load_with` would read freed memory. **Note 2026-10-27 (M4-P4 integration fixer C; Codex B1-1, CX-C-D1):** the instanceof hit walk takes 8 interrupt budget units after each full group of 8 chain steps and misses when the budget is used up; the cold path's generic walk then polls (H23 "Interrupt poll"). A walk that returns inside a group takes no unit for that group, so the last 1 to 8 steps of a walk take none, and a walk that uses up the budget runs up to 8 steps past it. The FREEZE-M1 batch-1 writer moves these into an H16 Freeze table. **Note 2026-10-28 (M4-P5 modes fixer; MODES-CHECK-w16 MC-w16-5):** the group size 8 (`WALK_STEPS`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/instance_ic.rs`) is a speed value (+1.2% instructions with a check per group, +7.2% with a check per step, on a monomorphic 20-deep walk). It is a tuning value in the doc 13 C-25 form, which freezes at the A4 gate, not with this cell at M1 exit. The rule is: at most one group of steps per walk goes uncharged (8 today), and a walk that uses up the budget misses after at most one group past it, so the generic walk polls |
| H16-Q4 | The index lists behind `intrinsic` and `builtin` refs | M1 (H13 lists) | H13 stable indices; BuiltinId = block (7 bits) + index (9 bits) |
| H16-Q5 | VM exporter (FeedbackVector → FactSnapshot). Split 2026-10-25 (V8-A21, M3-PLAN-D54): the `.ep9prof` container half is H16-Q5b | Month 2 (built M0 month 3) | Walk a live shape to its root to build the ShapeDesc. **Filled 2026-10-25 (M3-P3 FACTS-EXPORT): as proposed.** `ep9_vm::abi::export_facts(&Isolate, &UnitHash) -> Result<FactSnapshot, &'static str>` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs`; public by V8-A13, not in the H20 core) reads the first loaded unit with that content hash (else an error) and writes one record per feedback slot that a T0 IC site uses, in each function that has a vector: PropertyFacts for 0x21, 0x22, 0x24, 0x25 and GlobalFacts for 0x10-0x12. Other kinds get no record (the exporter reads only these kinds; T0 records the others since M4-P1 except InstanceOf, which it records from IC2 (M4-P3), H16-Q2, and FACTS2 exports them in M4-P2), and a function with no vector gets none. Header: this build's bytecode ABI, the unit hash, source `runtime`; `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1. IC state = the K3 entry state. Handlers: own field → `own-field`, `ARRAY_LENGTH` → `array-length`, `SLOW` → `generic`, `PROTO_LOAD` → `proto-field` (depth = chain steps to the holder), `MISSING` → `missing` (depth = prototypes to null), `ADD` → `add-field`; a global case gives `lexical`, `data-property` or (a `SLOW` global-object case) `accessor-property`. [Revised 2026-10-25, M3-P3 fix round 2, Codex C-FX1: the binding comes from what made the case, not from the key's prototype, because the global object can have a null prototype too. A field case is `lexical` and a GLOBAL cell `data-property`. A `SLOW` case is `accessor-property` for an accessor (a lexical binding is never one) and `data-property` for a data property under a shape whose prototype is not null; under a null prototype it is `lexical` or `data-property` only if its key is the current shape of the lexical record or of the global object (not both), else it gives no binding.] [Added 2026-10-25, M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 review: a `SLOW` case whose key is a dictionary-mode shape gives no binding, also for an accessor: the name can be anywhere in a dictionary table, so `Exporter::global` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs`) reads no `SLOW` case of a dictionary shape. A probe (not committed): a function that reads a global accessor gives `accessor-property`; when the global object is a dictionary (a `delete` of a property that is not its last one, `props::to_dictionary`), the same read gives no binding.] A live shape becomes a ShapeDesc by its root walk: its keys in property order with their attributes (the descriptor prefix of the shape, equal to the parent chain); its prototype is `null`, an intrinsic of the current realm by its `IntrinsicId` value (until the H16-Q4 lists), the initial `prototype` of a bytecode function (by unit hash and function id), or `unknown`. [Revised 2026-10-25, M3-P3 fix round 2, Codex C-FX2: `function-prototype` needs a proof that the VM keeps, not the `constructor` and `prototype` backlinks, which JS can write (`C.prototype = {constructor: C}`, an arrow function given a `prototype`). Only a class constructor has one: DefineClass makes its own `prototype` {w: false, c: false} and sets its `[[HomeObject]]` to that object, and no JS can change either. A `function` declaration's lazy `prototype` is writable and the VM keeps no proof of the initial object, so it is `unknown`, until a VM change keeps one.] No ShapeId, address or closure identity is written. [Added 2026-10-25, Codex C-FX3: the exporter keeps no table keyed by a heap-cell Value (H12-Q2 (a)); it describes each shape and prototype again where a case names it.] [Added 2026-10-25, M3-P3 fix round 3, Codex C2-FX1: a finished ShapeDesc is interned by its content (a set of `Arc<ShapeDesc>`, no heap address in the key), so equal descriptions share one `Arc` and the memory kept grows with the distinct descriptions, not with the sites. Test `equal_descriptions_share_one_arc` in `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs`: 256 sites that read one shape with a 64 KiB key share one description.] A case with no description is left out (a state may have fewer cases than the IC had): the string key, a dead key, an object kind with no ObjectKind, a stale prototype case (epoch changed) or a dead holder or target, and receivers with one description but two accesses. Value TypeSets stay empty (H16-Q2). Not a GC point; runs no JS. Tests: `tests::facts_export` (5) in `crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/facts_export.rs` (each handler, states, left-out cases, the root walk, equal bytes from two isolates) and `richards_facts_round_trip` in `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs` (richards: export, encode, decode, equal; the validator drops nothing; a second isolate gives equal bytes). [Revised 2026-10-25, fix round 2: `tests::facts_export` has 7 tests (added: an old null-prototype `SLOW` key gives no binding; no cell-keyed table in the exporter), and `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs` has 3 (added, each with and without GC stress: a null-prototype global's accessor and read-only cases are not `lexical`; only a class gives `function-prototype`, and the replaced, forged, arrow and `function`-declaration cases give `unknown`). Richards makes its objects with `function` declarations, so its receivers now have `unknown` prototypes.] The CLI flag `--facts-out` is M3-P4 FIXES |
| H16-Q5b | The `.ep9prof` container: the file that a PGO training run writes (`--profile-out`) and AOT reads | A3 (docs/plan/02-architecture.md:227: PGO capture is A3) | A container of `ep9_facts` snapshot bytes (`FactSnapshot::encode`), one per unit hash, with source `profiled`; decided with the A3 PGO capture |

## CacheIR op lists (V9-D9)

Filled 2026-10-27 (M4-P2 ADR-A0; plan v9 V9-D9, OI-10). The plan row asks for "CacheIR-like guard/action lists" (Decision). The form:

- **T0 keeps its handlers.** The int32 handlers and the IC_HANDLER and POLY_IC cells of K9-K18 stay the T0 form, so a T0 hit stays one compare and one load (K9). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-5: was "T0 never runs an op list".] T0 runs no op list today; a later list reader in T0 or GenInterp is a fill.
- **The op list is the shared form for JIT, AOT and facts.** A reader that needs more than T0 (T1, T2, unit code, the facts exporter, the stub cache) maps one IC case to one list with one total function, `ops_of(case, operation)`: guards first, then one action. The operation is the site's (load, store or define, from the site opcode), because one handler kind can serve more than one: a `GLOBAL` case serves the loads 0x10, 0x11 and the store 0x12 (`ic::global_case(rt, name, store)`; `ic::store_global` writes through it). Each pair of handler kind and operation maps to a fixed list, so the list adds no RW state. [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 Codex-fix docs agent, P2-ORCH-9 (Codex round 1, ADR-A0 slice): was `ops_of(case)`, one list per handler kind.]
- **Refs, not pointers.** A list names heap things through the H30 ref nodes (an append-only list in H30; today the five RootRef, IntrinsicRef, ShapeRef, CellRef, UnitConst) [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P4 integration fixer A, MC-w15-10a: was "the five H30 ref nodes"], never through a raw address, so the same list serves JIT finalize and unit finalize (H34). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-3: was "(ShapeRef, CellRef, RootRef)".] The FunctionRef, ProtoRef, holder ref and record ref of the ops below are names (FunctionRef and ProtoRef are the H16 FactSnapshot forms), not ref nodes of their own: the ref node or H34 relocation that each one lowers to is fixed with the first reader that emits it (a fill).
- **Append-only.** Ops and handler kinds form append-only lists ([13 §5.4](../plan/13-execution-modes.md#54-room-in-the-contracts-append-only-parts-and-gated-parts), C-24): a new op or a new handler kind is a fill when its first reader is built; an existing op never changes its meaning; a reader that meets an unknown op treats the case as a miss (generic path).

| Op (v0 list) | Kind | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| GuardShape(obj, ShapeRef) | guard | word0 ShapeId of obj = the shape (the K9 compare) |
| GuardProtoValidity(ProtoRef) | guard | the prototype chain of the case is still valid: in T0 the handler's VALIDITY_CELL (K5); in compiled and unit code a Dynamic FuseRef of the descriptor kind "prototype-chain validity by ProtoRef" (H31-Q1), never the cell itself, because an invalid cell stays invalid and the relearned case gets a new one (H17 validity row). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-1: was GuardValidity(CellRef).] |
| GuardKind(obj, kind) | guard | the cell kind byte (array, string; later an elements kind, H18) |
| GuardType(value, int32 / number / string / symbol / bigint) | guard | the value's type (BinaryOp, CompareOp, keyed key types) |
| GuardTarget(callee, FunctionRef) | guard | the call target (Call, InstanceOf) |
| GuardIndex(obj, key) | guard | an in-bounds index, not a hole (keyed) |
| GuardSlotsRoom(obj, location) | guard | the object's SLOTS cell exists and has room at the location (an out-of-line ADD: T0 stores only into room the SLOTS cell has, else it misses, `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs` `store`, the ADD case). [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-2] |
| GuardNotHole(value) | guard | a lexical binding is not the TDZ hole (the lexical-record case of a global site: T0 misses on the hole). [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P2 integration fixer B, review R-CI-4] |
| LoadField(obj, location) / StoreField(obj, location) | action | an in-object or SLOTS field (FIELD_IN_OBJECT, FIELD_OUT_OF_LINE) |
| LoadHolderField(holder ref, location) | action | a field of a prototype holder (PROTO_LOAD) |
| AddField(obj, ShapeRef, location) | action | the K13 add: store, then the new shape (H21 P3 order) |
| LoadArrayLength / LoadStringLength | action | ARRAY_LENGTH, STRING_LENGTH |
| LoadUndefined | action | MISSING |
| LoadGlobalField(record ref, location) | action | GLOBAL (with GuardShape on the global object and the lexical record) |
| StoreGlobalField(record ref, location) | action | GLOBAL at a store (0x12 `StaGlobal`): one store of the value into the global object's field, through the write barrier (H11), as `ic::store_global` does (`object::wr`). A store case exists only for a writable data property: `ic::global_case` makes a read-only one `SLOW`. [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P2 Codex-fix docs agent, P2-ORCH-9] |
| LoadElement(kind) / StoreElement(kind) | action | keyed, per elements kind (KEYED, M4-P3; H18) |
| CallTarget(FunctionRef) / CallGetter / CallSetter | action | monomorphic calls; accessor ICs (H17 Accessor ICs row) |
| Generic | action | SLOW and MEGA: the generic operation (megamorphic: the stub cache, H16-Q3) |

| Handler (T0 form) | `ops_of` |
|---|---|
| FIELD_IN_OBJECT, FIELD_OUT_OF_LINE | GuardShape, LoadField or StoreField |
| PROTO_LOAD | GuardShape, GuardProtoValidity, LoadHolderField |
| MISSING | GuardShape, GuardProtoValidity, LoadUndefined |
| PROTO_LOAD, MISSING under `KEY_STRING` (a string's method load; M4-P3 IC2, K11) | GuardKind (string), GuardProtoValidity (the chain from %String.prototype%), then LoadHolderField or LoadUndefined. GuardKind, not GuardShape: a string has no shape, and the kind check keeps a symbol (ShapeId 0 too) off the case. [Added 2026-10-27, M4-P3 integration fixer, IC2 need 3] |
| ADD | GuardShape, GuardProtoValidity (0x24 only), GuardSlotsRoom (out of line only), AddField |
| GLOBAL | GuardShape (global object), GuardShape (lexical record), then LoadGlobalField (load: 0x10, 0x11) or StoreGlobalField (store: 0x12). [Revised 2026-10-27, M4-P2 Codex-fix docs agent, P2-ORCH-9: was LoadGlobalField for every operation, so a store would have been compiled as a load] |
| smi field keyed on the lexical record (0x10-0x12) | GuardShape (lexical record), then LoadField and GuardNotHole on the value (load), or GuardNotHole on the old value and StoreField (store) |
| ARRAY_LENGTH / STRING_LENGTH | GuardKind, LoadArrayLength / LoadStringLength. T0 keys ARRAY_LENGTH on the array's shape; GuardKind is a deliberate widening (any ARRAY has its int32 `length` at +24), sound for every array shape |
| SLOW, MEGA | Generic |
| CONSTRUCT_SITE, CLONE, INSTANCEOF (IC_LAYOUT 2) | no list in v0: allocation-site and instanceof cases are read as facts (CallFacts, CloneFacts, TargetFacts) until a tier needs a list; a list for them is a fill |

The smi handlers are in `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs:136-227`, the IC_HANDLER kinds in `crates/ep9_vm/src/ic.rs:230-260`. Nothing is built: the first `ops_of` comes with its first reader (month 5-6 ICs, OI-15, or Spike C). The op list is not a FactSnapshot format, so it needs no `FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump.

## Fact meanings: count units, call keys, implicit int32 (F-R7, F-R8, F-R9)

Filled 2026-10-27 (M4-P2 Codex-fix docs agent; orchestrator decision P2-ORCH-12; [13 §4.3](../plan/13-execution-modes.md#43-rules) F-R7, F-R8, F-R9 and C-06). This is what M4-P2 FACTS2 built; the exporter module doc says the same (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:69-109`). No slot kind, schema or encoding changes (`FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1).

**Each count names its unit (F-R7; `crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:86-100`).** A merge adds counts of one unit only (H16-Q5b). Every count saturates at `i32::MAX`.

| Field | Unit | Writer |
|---|---|---|
| `CallFacts.count`, `Call*` site | sampled calls: a call in the first `CALL_WINDOW` (64) budget units after a budget reset | `profile.rs` |
| `CallFacts.count`, `Construct*` site | constructs of the cold path (a hit writes nothing) | `construct_ic.rs` |
| `CallFacts.count`, a slot that a `Call*` and a `Construct*` site share | none: the count is 0 | - |
| `LoopFacts.back_edges` | sampled back edges: a back edge that polls | `profile.rs` |
| `LiteralFacts.count` | cold-path literal results: a lower bound of the allocations | `literal_site.rs` |

A sampled count is a share of the budget units, not of the executions. The windows come at a fixed period, so a call pattern with that period can stay out of every window (test `a_periodic_call_pattern_keeps_sites_out_of_the_fixed_window` in `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs`); a jittered window start is a month-5 item. [Built 2026-10-28, M5-P1 PROFILE5: a poll resets the budget to `BUDGET` plus a jitter in 0..128 units from one fixed sequence (`profile::budget_after_poll`; every isolate and process runs the same sequence, so a second isolate gives the same facts), and a call is in the window when its budget before it is in (`BUDGET` - 64, `BUDGET`]. The test is now `a_periodic_call_pattern_reaches_every_site_through_the_jittered_window`: every one of the 99 sites gets a count.]

**A call key is never written out (F-R8; `crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:101-109`).** T0 keys a `Call*` site by the native word of the callee's FunctionInfo and a `Construct*` site by the constructor cell (weak). The exporter branches on the site opcode and looks the FunctionInfo up by its address; it never reads the word as a pointer. A function of a loaded unit (not function 0) becomes `FunctionRef::Js {unit hash, id}`, a table builtin of the current realm `FunctionRef::Builtin(id)`. Anything else (a host function, a bound function, a proxy) makes the site megamorphic with no target. A slot that a `Call*` and a `Construct*` site share is megamorphic with count 0 (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:401-414`). Tests `a_call_key_becomes_a_function_ref_or_megamorphic` and `a_shared_call_and_construct_slot_is_megamorphic` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/tests/facts_export.rs`).

**The keyed kinds set: every miss, hits only when sampled (IC_LAYOUT 3 bits 10-16; M5-P1 ELEM1; `crates/ep9_vm/src/profile.rs` `record_keyed_cold`, `keyed_hit_kind`).** The value half of a keyed slot holds in bits 10-16 one bit per elements kind (0-6, H18) of the receivers it saw before the access, for an index key on an Array or on an object of an `elements::plain_elements_kind` kind. Kinds 7-15 are reserved and append-only (`elements::kind`, OBJECT_LAYOUT 5; doc 13 F-R5): no kind uses them today, and the set holds no bit for them. Every miss records the kind (the cold body). A fast hit (the `GetKeyedProperty` array path, the StoreKeyed store in place) records it only in the sampled window of `record_call` (the first `CALL_WINDOW` budget units after a budget reset), and as `Flow::Record`, so the T0 handler keeps no frame. A monomorphic StoreKeyed hit records nothing: its shape holds the kind, and the miss that installed the shape recorded it. So the set holds every kind a miss saw and the kinds of the sampled hits; a kind that only hits outside every window see is missing, as an unsampled call target is. Recording at every hit cost about 3% (t0-prop), 10% (crypto) and 13% (navier-stokes) more instructions than no hit record (M5-P1 BENCH-SET, Mac a64). No IC hit reads these bits, so `ics_only` (no record hooks, MODES-D19) hits the same and leaves the set empty. Schema 1 exports none of them (`facts_export.rs` `keyed` reads the KeySet bits only); the month-6 bump decides (F-R5). Tests `tests::elements::keyed_slots_record_the_kinds_they_saw`, `tests::elements::the_store_hit_keeps_the_kind`.

**The keyed IC state is live only for StoreKeyed (MC-w15-1; `crates/ep9_vm/src/keyed_ic.rs`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs` `keyed`).** Only the StoreKeyed fast case (a present element of an Array) writes the key half of a keyed slot; the LoadKeyed, DefineKeyedOwn, HasKeyed and StoreInArrayLiteral bodies always go cold and leave it alone. So at those kinds, and at a StoreKeyed site that never took the fast case (an append, say), `Uninitialized` means "no state", not "never ran" (MODES-D17's "ran" bit is the only "never ran" signal), and a keyed `Monomorphic` (one receiver shape) exports no case: schema 1 has no element access to describe. The `ep9_facts` `IcState` and `KeyedFacts` docs say the same; test `keyed_ic_state_is_live_only_for_store_keyed_present_elements` in `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/facts_export.rs` pins it.

**An empty BinaryOp or CompareOp slot means "int32 or never ran" (F-R9, MODES-D10; `crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:69-85`).** T0 writes nothing in the int32 case (H16-Q2 (1)), and the exporter adds the INT32 bit to every record of the kind in a function that has a vector (`crates/ep9_vm/src/facts_export.rs:426-431`). A slot starts as int32 0, so a record whose only bit is INT32 cannot tell a cold site from an int32 site. AOT reads it that way; the H32 site budget bounds the cost when the site later sees other values. Two changes are decided:
1. MODES-D15: the one month-6 schema bump adds a per-site "ran" bit, written only inside the sampled window (no T0 cost outside it). AOT then compiles a never-ran site as generic and out of line, not as an int32 guard.
2. MODES-D16: the month-5 PROFILE step makes T0 record an int32 `+`, `-`, `Inc` or `Dec` that overflows (today the overflow stays implicit, H16-Q2 (1)). The write sits on the overflow path only, so the hit path does not change. Until then no fact backs an overflow guard of these four operations. [Built 2026-10-28, M5-P1 PROFILE5: T0 records `BinaryHint::INT32_OVERFLOW` ("int32 operands and a result outside int32", the bit `*`, `/` and `>>>` already write; not NUMBER, MC-w14-19) when an int32 `+` or `-` (`Add`, `Sub`, `AddSmi`, `SubSmi`, also in their `Wide` and `ExtraWide` forms) or an int32 `Inc` or `Dec` gives a result outside int32. The fast body returns the record from the branch the checked operation takes on overflow (`binop_fast`, `Inc` and `Dec` in `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/ops.rs`; `profile::INT32_OVERFLOW`), so the int32 result that fits tests nothing more, and `cargo xtask disasm-check --all-targets` keeps the handler and frame lists of the base on 5 targets. This replaces two sentences of the H16-Q2 cell (1): "An int32 `+`/`-` that overflows stays implicit" and "A unary op records no overflow"; `Negate` still records no overflow (not a D16 operation). So a BinaryOp record of these four operations without `INT32_OVERFLOW` now means "no overflow seen, or never ran", and a consumer may use it as the basis of their overflow guard (Spike C still treats that guard as unbacked). No slot kind, schema or encoding change (`FACT_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 1). Tests: `tests::profile` (one per arm: `an_int32_add_that_overflows_records_int32_overflow` and the `sub`, `inc` and `dec` forms; `d16_results_that_fit_and_negate_stay_implicit`; binary cases 1 and 13 now expect `INT32_OVERFLOW`).]

## Reopens if

- SP-Facts misses its gate (09 §4).
- M1 needs a slot kind before the freeze: one bump, by the steward (bytecode ABI and fact schema together).
- Per-function feedback turns sites polymorphic much more often than V8 (H16-Q1).

| 1 Reviewer (M4-P4 amendment: the 2026-10-27 fixer C rewrite row, stub cache flush rule and InstanceOf hit budget) | M4-P4 records reviewer C (Claude; plan v9 rule 21, §6.7; wrote none of the change: no ADR text, no Change-log row, no drift pin, no M4-P4 code, test or golden, no Codex text; it also wrote row 3 of this set and, in round 2, corrected row 2's CX-C-D1 summary, records check C F2) | Checked at 5281c03. (1) Flush rule (Codex D2-1): the H16-Q3 fixer C note says the table is emptied at every collection, minor ones included, and that a table kept across a minor GC first needs a minor-GC weak filter of every cell an entry names. Code: `StubCache::new` registers `flush` as a weak source of the heap (`stub_cache.rs`, no change since 2e016d0); `collect_scanning` (heap.rs) calls `roots.visit_weak` for a minor and a major GC alike; `flush` sets every key to 0 when an entry went in since the last GC. The reason holds: `ic::record` makes the handler and then puts a megamorphic case in the table (`stub_insert`), so a new IC_HANDLER can be named by the table only, and `ic::load_with` reads that cell. `tests::stub_cache` (9, with `the_stub_cache_is_empty_after_every_gc`) passes in debug and `--release`. Plan: v9 §5.3 IC2 says flushed at every GC (the H16-Q3 default); the §6.7 MC-w15-7a item asked for 'at least at every major GC'; the orchestrator's D2-1 input (fixC-inputs) chose to keep 'flush at every collection', which the note does, so the plan's -7a wording is superseded ((c), a plan note for the plan owner). (2) InstanceOf hit budget (B1-1; the text as 319f303 left it): `instance_ic::hit` walks in groups of `WALK_STEPS` (8): the inner loop returns TRUE (a match), FALSE (the end of the chain) or a miss (a proxy) with no unit, and only after a full group does `*budget -= WALK_STEPS as i64` run, with a miss when the budget is at or below 0. So a walk of 8 steps or fewer takes no unit, the last 1 to 8 steps of a longer walk take none, a walk that uses up a budget of 1 or more runs at most 7 steps past it, and one that starts at 0 or below runs 8 steps (`call_bytecode` in call.rs takes its unit at line 496, after its `poll` at line 470, so a callee can start at 0). A miss goes to the cold path: `property::instance_of` reaches `prototype_walk_step`, which takes a unit and polls at 0 or below at its first step. The note's 'up to 8 steps past it' covers both cases. Author evidence: fixer C measured a check per step at +7.2% and per 8 steps at +1.2% instructions (wb-inst-mono +1.17% against 930945e; BENCH-SET within 0.2%); verifier C repeated the numbers, and its mutation M8 (no budget in the hit walk) fails `a_termination_stops_the_hit_walk_of_a_warm_site_on_a_long_chain`, which passes at 5281c03 (`tests::instance_ic` 10, debug and `--release`). One left-over outside the ADR at 5281c03: the doc comment of that test said the hit walk 'takes one interrupt budget unit per step' (a code comment; records reviewer C report D1); fixed in 12ab1e5 (comment only, records check C F1). Kind rewrite (proposed; pre-freeze churn; Accepted path): agree: the flush rule rejects again what the 7a note accepted, so a decided statement changes; H16 freezes at M1 exit, so G8 does not count it; the hit-budget note adds to the IC2 text and changes no state, layout or fact. The inner loop returns TRUE (a match), FALSE (the chain's end) or a miss (a proxy) with no unit; only after a full group does `*budget -= WALK_STEPS as i64` run, with a miss at or below 0. So at most one group of a walk (its last 1 to 8 steps) goes uncharged, and a walk that uses up the budget misses after at most one group past it; the miss goes to `property::instance_of`, whose generic walk (`prototype_walk_step`) takes a unit and polls at its first step. The uncharged group is the last group of a walk that returns (a match or the chain's end: no generic walk follows) or misses on a proxy; the generic walk polls after a budget miss, which comes after a charged group. (1) Text: 03d0ddc changes 2 phrases in H16: the H16-Q3 note and this Change-log row. Both now say 'at most one group of steps per walk goes uncharged (8 today), and a walk that uses up the budget misses after at most one group past it, so the generic walk polls' (the row has no '(8 today)'). That is the text R-P5M-1 suggested, word for word. Since 29a095c no other H16 body text changed: `git diff 29a095c 6ebe361` has hunks only at the H16-Q3 cell, this Change-log row and the record rows (5ed8ec8, 281b843, c0b8552). (2) Code: no crate line changed since 4941d6a except comments (af8c9e9, in other files). In `instance_ic::hit` the inner loop of `WALK_STEPS` (8) steps returns TRUE (a match), FALSE (the chain's end) or a miss (a proxy) with no unit. Only after a full group does it take 8 units, and it misses at or below 0. So at most one group per walk goes uncharged: the last 1 to 8 steps of a walk that returns. A budget miss comes after a charged group, at most one group past the point where the budget ran out (8 steps when the budget is at or below 0 at entry: a call takes its unit after its poll, `call::call_bytecode`, so it can leave 0). The miss goes cold to `property::instance_of`. Its walk calls `prototype_walk_step`, which takes 1 unit and polls when the budget is at or below 0, so it polls at its first step. The new text is true at 6ebe361. (3) Plan: plan v9 §7 step 1 (MODES-CHECK-w16) gives MC-w16-1..5 to P5-MODES, and w16 MC-w16-5 asks for one clause in H16 and H23. Done; the ADR text drops the 'then' of the w16 wording, as R-P5M-1 asked. The Codex round 2 docs slice read H16 at 4e0cca8, after 03d0ddc: 'No substantive mismatch found in the reviewed H16/H23 claims.' (4) Kind clarify (proposed; Accepted path): agree. 03d0ddc is text only, in an Open-question cell, not a Rules row; no check, test, golden or value changes its result. R-P5M-1: closed. (3) The code at e4e89ce (no `crates/` line other than comments changed since 4941d6a). `instance_ic::hit` walks in groups of `WALK_STEPS` (8). A return inside a group (TRUE, FALSE, or a miss on a proxy) takes no unit. After a full group it takes 8 units and misses at or below 0. The miss goes cold: `compare_cold` -> `slow::compare` -> `property::instance_of`, whose walk calls `prototype_walk_step`: 1 unit, and a poll at or below 0, at its first step. `call_bytecode` polls (`call.rs:470`) before it takes its unit (`call.rs:496`), so a walk can start at 0; it then misses after one group of 8. So the note is true: at most one group per walk goes uncharged (8 today), and a walk that uses up the budget misses after at most one group past it, so the generic walk polls. The value is unchanged: `const WALK_STEPS: usize = 8;`. | 1 Reviewer (M5-P1 amendment: the 2026-10-28 rewrite row, sampled-call window jitter, PROFILE5) | M5-P1 Claude reviewer (Claude; plan v10 rule 21 (c), §3.9; wrote none of the change: no ADR text, Change-log row, code, test or Codex text; review part 1 at 320a29b, records part 2 at 7c70de6) | Checked at 7c70de6 (the row came with the wire patch d9f213c, after part 1). (1) `interp::slow::interrupt` (`slow/mod.rs:133`) is the poll reset, and it sets `cx.budget = profile::budget_after_poll(rt)`: `BUDGET` plus the low 7 bits of a mixed state, so 0..127 units (`CALL_JITTER` 128 = 2 x `CALL_WINDOW`, a power of two by a const assert); the state is per isolate (`Runtime::call_window_jitter`) and starts from one fixed constant, so every isolate and process gets the same sequence; `JITTER_OFF` gives the fixed reset. T0 polls, the native entries (`native.rs` 347, 361, 841, 1116) and `Cx::poll_interrupt` (`abi/index.rs:95`) all reach that function. `record_call` records only for a budget in (`BUDGET` - 64, `BUDGET`]: the lower bound on the hot path, the upper bound after `cold_path()`; `record_call_pushed` tests the upper bound too. (2) `tests::profile` 24 pass; `cargo test -p ep9_builtins --test facts_export` 7 pass with `a_periodic_call_pattern_reaches_every_site_through_the_jittered_window`. (3) Kind test applied (README §3.3 rewrite: 'a value that code, data or a file format depends on changes'; a test is replaced, so an existing test result changes): rewrite. Author: rewrite (proposed). Confirmed. Open (b): (i) the CI step 'spike-native tests (ep9_vm)' (`ci.yml:240-242`, `cargo test -p ep9_vm --features spike-native --locked native`) fails 2 tests at 7c70de6: `tests::native::a_native_callee_whose_frame_does_not_fit_takes_one_unit` and `a_native_callee_whose_frame_fits_takes_one_unit` ('AotBaseline Undefined budget 1', left -38, right 4). They count units across a poll as `budget + (BUDGET - left)`, so the jitter adds its j units. With `call_window_jitter` set to `JITTER_OFF` in `run_native_overflow` (a temporary edit, reverted) both pass. `--features jit,spike-native --lib`: 638 pass, the same 2 fail. Fix: set `JITTER_OFF` there, as the `interp/ops.rs` budget tests do. (ii) `keyed_hit_kind` has no upper bound, so keyed hits record in the jitter units (the ELEM1 row above, Open (b)) | branch m1m5/p1-merge at 7c70de6, mac a64; logs scratchpad m1m5/p1/review (rec-vm.log, rec-facts-export.log, rec-ci-native.log, rec-vm-jit-native.log, rec-native-jitteroff-probe.log) |
